DOCUMENT: specs/MOTION_AND_CINEMATOGRAPHY.md
===========================================================================
# Motion language and cinematic interaction direction

## Creative direction
Mood: restrained architectural film, premium boutique property showroom, trustworthy contemporary Iraqi luxury. Slight camera weight, elegant easing, minimal UI. NOT cyberpunk, hyperactive TikTok transitions, glitch overlays, oversaturated neon, random bouncing elements or loud whooshes. Motion serves spatial context. Brand reveal establishes architecture and orientation.

## Camera choreography
Use actual camera pose structs {position,target,fov,roll,near,far,duration,ease,sceneId}. Avoid generic hardcoded fake orbit around invisible target. Author and validate poses for: Hero 3/4, front, facade close, selected level, exploded level, aerial unit, living eye-level, kitchen, master, bath, balcony, pool entry, gym, restaurant dusk, rooftop terrace, final wide. For each transition implement end-state reconciliation when interrupted, reversed or viewport changes.
- Hero reveal: subtle transition from warm mist/light into architectural 3/4 view, brand typography emerges only after useful image; user can skip.
- Floor hover: 100–250ms responsive material/outline, no camera hijack. Floor click: 0.8–1.8s smooth focus/extraction when motion allowed, fast if repeated taps.
- Floor to dollhouse: controlled cutaway and easing, maintain orientation and unit center. Avoid clipping through walls; if layout doesn't allow a collision-free continuous flight, use a masked match-cut, never fake impossible movement.
- Room to room: interpolation with collision map or deliberate architectural cut; ensure doorway/windows aligned; no rapid roll/pitch.
- Material swap: near-instant (<500ms perceived after cached texture) surface update with restrained crossfade if no PBR artifact; inspector transitions independent of camera.
- Gallery: 250–500ms image change, no heavy GPU effects; swipe/keyboard cues.
- Guided tour: distinct scenes, optional original ambient audio with user-triggered playback and mute controls. No copyrighted audio from reference reel; do not auto-play audio.

## Technical motion rules
Use one timeline owner for critical route transitions, event sequencing finite-state machine. Cancel current tween on new input, maintain target and camera consistency; clean up GSAP contexts/listeners. On-demand Three render loop when idle. Respect `prefers-reduced-motion`; preserve immediate navigation and comparable content for those users. Test scroll wheel, trackpad, touch swipe, native vertical scroll and tab focus. Avoid scroll-jacking on mobile and special keyboard gestures.

## Filmic composition QA
The tower must read against skyline at desktop 16:9 and phone 9:16 without cropping the crown/balconies. Text and model never collide; annotations avoid window frames/hotspots. Warm/cool color balance, stable horizon and shadow direction. Artifact inspection: screenshot at 0/25/50/75/100% each major transition; no flickering geometry, accidental hide, camera inside walls, clipped panels, disorienting reverse motion.

## Audio and accessibility
Optional ambient score only if original/licensed; no sound required for comprehension; captions for narrated guided tour if any. Interactive sound effects subtle, optional and entirely muteable. Screenreader announces selected floor/unit/material without spamming on hover; keyboard access maps all camera-independent choices.



===========================================================================
DOCUMENT: specs/ENGINEERING_ARCHITECTURE.md
===========================================================================
# Software architecture and implementation contract

## Decision process, not rigid shopping list
Default candidate: Next.js or Vite React + TypeScript, Three.js + React Three Fiber + Drei, GSAP, CSS architecture with Tailwind/CSS variables, Zustand or reducer for domain state, Zod for structured validation, i18n with Arabic RTL and English, Playwright for E2E, Vitest for pure logic, Blender Python + glTF. Claude researches *current compatible versions* and decides. Do not mindlessly combine Next.js server features with client-only rendering or ship engine libraries not necessary. Prefer stable supported versions, lockfile, deterministic installs.

## Proposed modular package topology
/src/app or pages (routes, localized shell)
/src/components/navigation (menu, breadcrumbs, controls, drawers)
/src/components/three (canvas, lights, camera rigs, meshes, rays, selection)
/src/components/exterior (building scene, hover, floor picker, xray)
/src/components/floors (floorplate, apt selectors)
/src/components/interior (rooms, hotspots, camera presets, movement)
/src/components/configurator (material inspector, swatches, packages, history)
/src/components/gallery (consistent gallery, captions)
/src/components/amenities (wellness, sky dining)
/src/components/summary (finish board, PDF/print, contact demo)
/src/domain (project, floor, room, unit, surface, material, selection, validation)
/src/state (navigation finite-state, persisted selection)
/src/i18n, /src/lib, /src/styles, /src/fixtures, /tests
/public/assets (models, textures, renders, thumbnails, posters)
/tools/blender (source model and generation scripts)
/project_artifacts (research, QA evidence, checkpoint logs)

## Typed data contracts
Project: id, name, locationTextConcept, levelIds, brand, conceptOnly, architectureVersion, heroAssets.
Floor: id/level/label, category{ground,residential,wellness,dining}, elevationMeters, geometry IDs, units[] or zones[], availabilityDemo, amenities[], hotspots[].
Unit: id/floorId/typeId, bedrooms, bathrooms, nominalAreaSqm, areaBasis{gross,net,unknown}, roomIds[], planAssetId, cameraBookmarkIds, displayStatusDemo.
Room: roomId/unitId, labelAr/en, polygonMeters[], height, openingIds, surfaceIds[], cameraPose, galleryAssetIds.
Surface: surfaceId, sceneMeshNode, roomId, materialCategory, allowedMaterialIds[], baseMaterialId, uvScalePhysical, usageZone, specificationMetadata.
Material: materialId, catalogCategory, nameAr/en, appearance, pbrAssets, thermal/slip/microbial/fire fields verifiedOrUnknown, provenance, license, physicalFit.
Asset: id, src, type, byteEstimate, provenance, rights, version, semanticMapping, fallbackAssetId.
Selection: activeProject, floor, unit, room, surface, materialOverrides{}, selectedPackage, lighting, viewMode, language, schemaVersion; never persist credentials.

## State transitions and validation
App phases: landing, building, selectedFloor, floorPlan, unitDollhouse, roomInterior, materialInspector, gallery, amenity, summary. Each has allowed events and guard conditions. No unitSelected if floor is amenity; no bath-only tile on kitchen counter; invalid floor IDs fall back to safe landing. Serialize selection/version with migrations, validate payload with schema on restore and sharing. User can use Back, breadcrumb and reset without stale camera state. Model load states idle/loading/success/error with retries; disable actions dependent on unloaded model but keep data view available.

## WebGL lifecycle
DPR dynamic/quality tiers, contextual loading and GPU memory budgets, scoped material changes and texture cache, shared geometries, proper disposal/unmount, data-driven raycast target maps, no rendering after unmount, error boundary, context lost/recovered handling, import checks and 2D fallback. Never couple persisted selections solely to transient Three mesh references.

## Responsive localization
Arabic RTL root and localized components with LTR handling for Latin identifiers and measurements, no reversed numeric IDs. Content keys for every interface and alt text; suitable fonts properly licensed and bundled. On phones, 3D takes top with bottom sheet (not tiny sidebar), explicit selectable floor list, pinch zoom without scrolling conflict, safe areas, no hover-only info. Desktop 1440/1080, laptops 1366, tablets 768, phones 390/360 widths; test actual captured screenshots.

## Storage/backend and demo truthfulness
Start client-only curated demo or minimal demo server. No database required to fake sales operations. Local storage opt-in or explicit Save, print-to-PDF and link share. For future commercial product, propose separate backend and CMS/CRM as Phase 2 with auth, data lifecycle, audit logs. Never invent messages "Inquiry sent" if no endpoint; show "Demo recorded locally / not delivered". Do not store personal contact fields without consent; avoid transmitting to third-party services.

## Technical discipline
Define coding standards/ESLint/Prettier/tsc, package versions, documented commands, typed errors, component tests, Playwright flows, accessible tests, no production debug overlays. Treat compiler+browser results as evidence. Fail closed on invalid asset IDs; no eval, no HTML injection, no hardcoded third-party secrets. CI scripts if Git available. Branches/commits only inside project; ask before remote push or deployment.

DOCUMENT: specs/TECHNICAL_DEEP_DIVE.md
===========================================================================
# Engineering deep dive: practical edge cases, system boundaries, exact contracts

## Layout and route state
The final experience may use a single shell and multiple nested route screens. Regardless of router strategy, deep links must express at least locale, floor, unit and room if appropriate. Visiting an unknown unit ID may redirect to floor selection with a nonblocking message. URL and local saved state must reconcile under deterministic precedence: explicit URL > approved persisted selection > app defaults. All values must be validated, and no route should crash when GLB hasn't loaded. UI should never render a unit for an amenity floor.

## Semantic architecture of scenes
Build a SceneRegistry keyed by sceneId and a FloorNodeIndex keyed by floorId. Each raycastable building level has a stable semantic ID, even if geometry optimized/instanced. Selection lights the correct floor or region and no other. Provide fallback DOM controls with exact same state transitions as 3D mesh clicks. When selected, highlight must remain noticeable in sunlight/dark scenes; use subtle edge/outlining, measured colors, no eye-searing neon. Scene focus target from predefined spatial metadata rather than naïve world origin. Balcony or interior hotspots should not be occluded by noninteractive transparent glass during raycasting; maintain separate hitbox layers.

## Precise camera behavior
Set camera min/max distance based on building bounding box and viewport aspect ratio. Use camera focus and orbit control ownership rules; only one system controls camera at a time. GSAP motion transitions can take ownership, abort old animation, and return to user control. Prevent jitter if mouse wheel scroll occurs while floor extraction animates. On restore/back, re-establish camera target and filtered pointer event handlers. Handle 90-degree screen orientation changes; target remains framed. Always offer Reset Camera with meaningful context (current scene bookmark rather than jump to default exterior). Add "Show Whole Floor" and "Look at selected room" actions that work even if pointer raycast selection failed.

## Three.js performance
Avoid allocating geometry, textures and materials on every frame and avoid React setState inside useFrame. Pool reusable material arrays; use mesh instancing for building repetitive facade components but not at cost of losing semantic floor selectable structure. Group static geometry into per-floor meshes for selection. Use LOD on furniture and facade small details; dynamic DPR and postprocessing only on high-performance devices; measure and tune rather than choosing aggressive fixed budgets. Favor shadow maps only for major key lights or baked shadows. Explicit sRGB output and toneMapping to approximate offline renders; HDR env exposure should be calibrated. When moving between multiple heavy scenes, ensure renderer textures/materials are correctly cached/disposed. Provide device-based selector quality and optional "Low graphics" toggle.

## glTF source-of-truth
Blender uses Z-up, Three/glTF often Y-up; exports need coordinate conversion and consistent unit scale. Keep object-level semantic identifiers in names or extras, not only fragile mesh ordering. Asset version hash changes invalidate stale indexes. In the build pipeline, glTF validator and scene manifest tests cross-check named surfaces, floor count, target bounding boxes and camera poses. Inspect materials converted from Blender Principled BSDF; know which nodes or procedural textures do NOT export. Bake complex procedural surfaces where necessary, with licensed texture sets and correct UV orientation. If using compression, bundle compatible decoders. Never ship dev-only 4K textures to every visitor; choose mipmaps and sensible resolution.

## Material swap implementation
A selectable surface is not identical to a material texture. A unit contains surfaces like living.floor.center, living.wall.north, living.panel.feature, kitchen.cabinet.fronts etc; each maps to one or more mesh nodes. Changing only the *selected surface* is default; "apply to whole apartment" must be explicit with a preview of changed slots. Avoid broad `.traverse()` replacing all materials named 'Floor' indiscriminately. Cache texture sets. For parquet/wood, rotate UV to follow realistic plank direction, true realistic board widths and visible seams; for tile show realistic 60x120 vs 60x60 look if size metadata claims so. Roughness and normal maps should visually distinguish stone, wood, painted plaster, not merely swatch hue. Avoid false physically measured properties.

## Form and data integrity
Use no real booking backend in fictional demo. A real-sales future integration may require admin login, CSRF protections, backend validation, anti-spam, email and GDPR/Iraqi privacy considerations; these are Phase 2 proposals. Client-side form accepts text only with robust length limits and avoids HTML injection. Consent required for local persistence if personal contact data, though better avoid storing contact data. Tests check intentionally malicious strings render harmlessly and do not execute. PDF documents contain chosen materials, issue time and fictional disclaimer. Any screenshot capture should crop out user identifying personal info.

## Arabic RTL implementation
Use semantic CSS logical properties; set `dir=rtl` for Arabic and preserve LTR isolated IDs (`U04A`, `125 m²`, `PDF`). Breadcrumb order, drawer origin, chevron direction, carousel swipe semantics, button icons and text alignment must be consistent, with Arabic numerals chosen deliberately. Font stack supports actual Arabic glyph shaping. Mixed Arabic/English room names preserve natural reading order. Input telephone numbers and emails remain LTR. Make sure numeric floor lists never visually reverse 01-11 ordering arbitrarily.

## Accessibility alternatives to canvas interactions
A Three Canvas is not semantically navigable by screenreader by default. Build an HTML list or accessible overlay matching all clickable floors, units and rooms. Keyboard actions and focus indicators are on DOM controls; camera may animate visually but selection state is not dependent on pointer. Visible labels show highlight and selection status in text. Every hot interaction can be achieved using DOM controls. Menus use `aria-expanded`, dialogs manage focus and Escape, status feedback is concise. Skip-to-content link and navigation landmarks appropriate. Reduced motion and "pause animated tour" required. Screenreader should receive a useful room/material description even if it cannot perceive the 3D scene.

## Render quality and web photo integrity
Each render may be a true Blender image with color grading, output metadata and camera angle, but still must clearly be labeled as conceptual render. User-selected materials may not match an earlier pre-render, requiring either correct paired package photo, true WebGL capture or explicit representative disclaimer. Technical metadata says `renderedPackageId`, `sourceSceneRevision`, `matchesActiveSelection:boolean`. If no match, the UI cannot show "Your final selected room" title. Capturing WebGL via preserveDrawingBuffer may hurt performance; choose safe screenshot pipeline and test browser contexts. Photo gallery should remain accessible if 3D renderer crashes.

## Fullscreen and mobile interactions
Phone users shouldn't accidentally pinch zoom webpage instead of scene or become stuck in orbit mode. Make interaction affordance and gesture instructions; use touch-action policy scoped to canvas, keep page scroll outside. Fullscreen gallery traps focus correctly, closes with X/back/Escape, and returns focus to launching thumbnail. Bottom drawers adapt to virtual keyboard and viewport safe areas. Landscape tablet image doesn't hide controls under nav bar. Quality toggles with data usage disclaimers (assets load lazily).

## Architectural consistency tests
Run checks: every floor elevation is monotonic; exactly 9 residential levels and 18 units; building facade level count matches data; unit room polygon boundaries don't overlap circulation illegally; balcony appears with proper exit from living; bedroom windows are placed in actual exterior walls; bath and kitchen plumbing shafts plausibly grouped; windows do not land inside columns; stair shafts align vertically; pool/wellness placement consistent across architecture and tour; dining roof line consistent in all renders.

## Operational maintenance and extensibility
Architectural sources must be editable. Provide tooling for updating a unit floorplan and regenerating 3D/proxy geometry plus camera/mesh metadata; document manual artist corrections when needed. Document material catalog import/export, image manifest update, translation keys and unit statuses. Avoid hardwired one-off U04A strings throughout UI; data-driven. Create a future migration plan for multi-project real estate CMS with company-level tenant isolation, proper auth and change logs, but do not implement unsafe fake admin authority in demo.

## Observability and incident handling
Centralized app-level errors and optional local debug panel in dev only; silence meaningless console spam. Failed GLB shows fallback/retry, not infinite spinner. Interaction loading state surfaced, e.g. "Loading apartment interior". Error reports do not expose personal data or secrets. If an asset format unsupported, select appropriate fallback; if KTX2 shader decode fails, offer plain WebP variant or conservative material; avoid blank screen. Show tested browser/GPU details in delivery report.

## Visual fidelity escalation plan
If exterior currently looks generic, address geometry massing + window proportions before adding fancy shaders. If materials look plastic, fix PBR maps/roughness/sRGB/exposure. If rooms look fake, fix geometry scale/furniture/window/light/shadow. If visual output resembles a PowerPoint slideshow, restore spatial navigation and continuous 3D controls. If 3D is too slow, hybrid should preserve meaningful spatial selection/live material swapping while precalculating hero photos. Prioritize aesthetic deficits that influence a company's first 30 seconds, without losing core functionality.

# Identity, art direction, microcopy and bilingual content

Brand is a fictional Basra-inspired premium project named Nakhla Residence / نخلة ريزيدنس. All rights retained by project author. No use of real Kabta or any developer's logos or copyrighted renderings. Logo should be original vector typographic + subtle palm-derived mark with minimalist construction (not generic cliché filled palm clip art).

Typography: elegant modern Arabic body and display paired with clean Latin typography, license checked. Aim for subtle headlines, restrained upper-case only in English, good typographic line-height in Arabic, legible captions, honest information density. Color palette conceptual: warm ivory #EBE2D2, charcoal bronze #252523, olive-sand #898477, muted bronze #9D8060, near-black #131716, warm-white #FAF7F1. Adjust by visual test/contrast; do not hardcode color where it breaks AA contrast.

Sample microcopy:
- "نخلة ريزيدنس — فخامة تنتمي للبصرة" / "NAKHLA RESIDENCE — Luxury rooted in Basra".
- "استكشف المشروع" / "Explore the Residence".
- "اختر طابقك" / "Select a Floor".
- "تفاصيل التصميم" / "Explore the Interior".
- "غيّر التشطيب" / "Customize Finishes".
- "قارن الخيارات" / "Compare Finishes".
- "احفظ تصميمك" / "Save Your Design".
- "جولة سينمائية" / "Guided Tour".
- "نموذج تصوري غير مرتبط بعقار معروض للبيع" / "Concept demonstration — not a real property listing".

Narrative: inviting but evidence-based. Amenities/luxury features label as conceptual design, not presently available services. Do not mention true area approvals or certification without evidence. Feature-level data may change during architectural QA, maintain consistency with shared schema.

Art direction: architecture fills most of screen; UI recedes into minimal rail, frosted panel only if readability needs, no generic template/dashboard aesthetic, no fake overlarge glowing CTA. Design for high-end realtor sales office demo on large monitor as well as phone browsing.

Bilingual QA: Arabic transitions, pluralization, numbers, sqm and units, date formats; ensure selection ID "04A" consistent; menu, forms, PDF and labels all localized, not just hero. No arbitrary Iraqi dialect in legal/safety notices; clean modern Arabic for broad audience.

DOCUMENT: specs/ACCEPTANCE_AND_RELEASE_GATES.md
===========================================================================
# Phase gates and definition of done

Gate 0 — Evidence-based reconnaissance: environment inventory, screenshots/video inspected, original assets inventoried, licenses identified, skill security review performed, official documentation referenced, no duplicate installs, budget clear.
Gate 1 — Decision-grade research: at least 3 defensible architectural/site options and technical architecture alternatives considered, sources dated, assumptions labeled, major red flags in risk log, chosen solution with ADR and rationale. Do not spend endless time researching: stop when incremental information ceases to change the decision.
Gate 2 — Architecture geometry: tower exactly G+01–11, geometry stack consistent, two units for floors 01–09 only, amenity zones correct, conceptual square meters identified, windows align interior, unique clickable IDs, basic cutaway validated.
Gate 3 — Visual quality: photoreal-ish original/legit exterior hero and at least one stunning living room, believable room dimensions, realistic light and PBR, usable mobile poster; 3D rendering used where feasible, no flat cube pseudo-production assets. Visual comparison screenshots reviewed and improved.
Gate 4 — Functional core: floor hover and keyboard list, selection, camera focus and reversal, apartments and amenities, dollhouse->room transition, 6 classes of material inspector, live swappable materials, persistence and reset, day/night and xray or honest fallback, gallery, demo request/summary.
Gate 5 — Delight and consistency: guided tour interruptible, transitions don't fight controls, honest gallery/package synchronization, high polish, dark/warm tasteful typography, responsive controls.
Gate 6 — Reliability/accessibility: tsc/lint/unit/e2e pass, WCAG-oriented audit, keyboard and mobile touch, reduced motion, 2D fallback, GPU context loss, no dead CTAs, no broken text. Arabic and English fully tested.
Gate 7 — Shipping: local site launches in fresh checkout, deterministic startup instructions, licenses/assets, original .blend sources or source-of-truth scripts, performance observations, screenshots/video capture proof, honest completion report with known limitations.

Milestone strategy (autonomous): vertical slice in this order: beautiful exterior + one clickable residential floor -> real apartment cutaway -> one hero living room with one real material swap -> full six-category configurator -> amenities -> export/summary -> polish. Resist building all pages as decorative shells before first working 3D slice. If engineering constraints require different order, document rationale.

Exit gate requirements: All P0 blocking issues resolved or explicitly surfaced with independent usable fallback; visual signoff comparative captures; architecture consistency automated assertions; no copyrighted/unsafe assets; no false claims; no required paid service; no critical known functional defect.

# Additional standout ideas — prioritized without derailing core MVP

P1 only after base functions work:
1. Seamless automatic 3D guided "Sales Presentation Mode" showcasing tower -> floor -> Type A -> living material swap -> wellness -> dining -> finish board, with pause/skip and actual timed camera shots.
2. Building X-Ray with truthful per-level volumes, core services and transparencies; dedicated touch-safe UI alternative. Do not invent unseen furnished rooms.
3. A/B split or swipe comparison between warm/pearl finish presets with identical camera and lighting; non-pointer toggle.
4. Time-of-day preview: sun movement/sky lighting (artistic approximation unless real geolocated site/orientation measured), room lights dimmer and night facade.
5. Furnished / unfurnished toggle, only if original model supports layer groups; extremely useful to show construction versus styled ambiance.
6. Two-unit comparison: see Type A and B plan, room count, concept area and render; clearly no real sales inventory.
7. Material Passport/Finish Board share as polished PDF with a full named sample list and illustrative nature of finishes.
8. Project cutaway "How it Works" mode: visible core vs apartment vs facilities, press labels.
9. Accessibility-friendly 2D plan with tappable room polygons; no essential content limited to hovering in 3D.
10. Performance and visuals toggle "Cinematic / Balanced / Data Saver" with transparent browser capability hint.
11. Multiple camera presets and orbit/auto-orbit stopped on pointer input; control resets.
12. Architectural sustainability storytelling cards about shading/reduced west glass and materials, explicitly conceptual not energy certification.

P2 candidate ideas for a paid version (never required for portfolio demo):
13. AR glTF USDZ model preview on supported devices only after compatible export and proper permissions.
14. Sales-room kiosk mode with local autoplay, idle reset and QR code to continue apartment on phone.
15. Multi-project property CMS, mapping BIM/IFC data to verified geometry, rev-control of floorplans and sales records.
16. Real availability statuses synced to property ERP and audited dates; price quotation versioning/currency and Arabic PDF.
17. Verified supplier product lookup, material procurement availability, SKU/product and pricing region by region.
18. Solar exposure heatmap for specific geolocated/oriented real site only after physically appropriate simulation.
19. Outdoor view projections using real surveyed location/terrain, requiring licensing and disclosure; avoid fiction made to look like verified panorama.
20. Interactive furniture package swaps with accurate scale, not false claim inventory included.
21. Unit locator in larger multi-tower complex with GPS/map and accurately cited live address.
22. Verified green/thermal performance certificates and buyer transparency dashboard after actual engineering verification.
23. Live private tours with sales representative, reversible camera handoff and privacy protections.
24. Client wishlists, favorites, appointment CRM, secure developer admin and per-organization settings.
25. Optional export of user's chosen material palette to external design software/interoperable format.
26. Multi-language support beyond AR/EN, selective localization, documented stakeholder accessibility testing.
27. Composed take-home video tour from actual scene cameras and original music; use Lemo-Opuscar if vetted/relevant, not as website dependency.
28. Tiny original 3D avatar/motion logo optional only if it supports brand identity and doesn't increase load.
29. Transparent cost-of-upgrade table based solely on developer-entered confirmed prices.
30. Developer version and content audit trail when units or finish catalog changes.

Selection rule: any enhancement must first satisfy direct business value, no extra costs, no degradation of core frame rates, mobile fallback, low cognitive load, appropriate licensing. Keep a ranked optional backlog and do not use optional features as a pretext to ship an unfinished core.

DOCUMENT: specs/COMPANY_DEMO_AND_SALES_NARRATIVE.md

# Company showcase delivery — how to make the concept commercially convincing

## Company demo mission
A property developer should understand the commercial value in five minutes: buyers can visualize before construction, discover floor and apartment layout, understand and switch finishes, see building amenities, reduce unnecessary sales questions, save interest and share with sales team. Claim this as a *potential workflow*, not quantified ROI unless measured.

## First 20 seconds
Be breathtaking but usable: strong real photorealistic exterior hero, brand and "Explore Building" action visible immediately, no forced wait or unreadable tiny fonts. Show geography visually inspired by Basra (palms, warm sun, urban massing); label setting as concept. One delightful floor hover and animated selection should show capability without needing explanation.

## 60-second walkthrough
Visitor selects 04, clicks 04A, watches cutaway reveal real rooms, enters living and clicks flooring to switch variant, notices material inspector metadata, camera stable and visual material change obvious. Language toggle can happen mid-flow without reset. This should be polished enough to screen record for a sales pitch.

## Five-minute detailed demo
In sequence: hero -> orbit and Day/Night -> Floor 04 -> apartment A/B comparison -> unit A cutaway -> living + kitchen + master -> materials in 3 categories -> finish packages and A/B compare -> rooftop wellness pool and gym -> cafe/restaurant -> Material Board PDF summary -> demo inquiry (clearly local only). Show analytics/CRM as future concept, not working feature.

## Trust signals
At every stage explain concept drawings/renders vs real built photographs. Make labels subtle but visible. No false "only 2 units left", fake buyers/testimonials, fake rating, fake developer accreditation, fake energy certification or unverified origin claims. Avoid using real company branding as association or endorsement. Include original concept/legal footer.

## Price and product packaging for a future agency (not in current website checkout)
Offer the finished demo as an example of customizable enterprise-style real estate platform. Commercial proposal variants: Showroom Basic (high-end exterior and floor selection), Interactive Premium (full apartment/material configurator), Enterprise (real property data, CMS/CRM, multiple towers). Do not put actual prices without market research, project costing and contracted scope. Preserve implementation modules so reusable across customers, reducing future custom-project effort.

## Delivery pack for prospective client
Optional promotional <60-second screen capture of working interface> recorded with original permitted music; one-page explainer showing functionality, pilot requirements (DWG/Revit/IFC/3D scans, finish catalogs, brand/plan rights, pricing, content approval), sample supported devices and expected asset production schedule only if estimates researched. Show user actual preview in browser for strongest persuasion, not just pre-rendered video.

# Precise art-director shot list and render expectations

## Required shot catalogue
SH01 Hero tower three-quarter, near sunset: entire 12-level structure visible, warm sunlight grazes stone fins, dark bronze reads as metal, palms for scale, logo and CTA overlay in browser (not baked in render).
SH02 Tower north/front architectural neutral daylight: truthful facade grids, same exact level count, no inconsistent balconies.
SH03 Night tower three-quarter: subtle façade illumination, warm lived-in apartments, restaurant crown warm, pool level visually distinct but no impossible water wall visible externally.
SH04 Lobby main entry, waist-height 28–40mm equivalent: double-height perception, true canopy axis, reception and lounge.
SH05 Top view Level 04 floorplate: distinct A/B volumes and central lift/stair core.
SH06 Unit 04A isometric furnished cutaway: sofa/dining + kitchen + bedrooms + baths aligned to actual plan.
SH07 Living entrance-wide: clear sofa/tv/window/balcony axis, see floor material and feature wood in one hero composition.
SH08 Living material macro: material roughness/tile seams and contact shadows under consistent warm sun.
SH09 Kitchen 3/4: cabinetry, countertop and backsplash visible so material changes demonstrably meaningful.
SH10 Master bedroom: feature timber wall, believable bed size and window daylight.
SH11 Bath: waterproof floor/wall tile PBR and fixtures, credible scale and lighting.
SH12 Balcony: practical railing, shade and conceptual Basra-inspired city backdrop, no false actual location views.
SH13 Wellness floor pool entry: real pool deck/water plane, natural reflections, humidity-aware architecture concept.
SH14 Gym wide view: equipment layout, height, circulation and view.
SH15 Spa/relaxation detail: warm ambient lighting and natural materials.
SH16 Restaurant dusk: usable dining tables, window skyline, subdued contrast.
SH17 Cafe bar warm: café counter, seating and high-end lighting.
SH18 Terrace viewpoint: panoramic deck and safe edge concept, separate hypothetical shisha zone with legal note.
SH19 Material passport editorial composition, six swatches and ID labels as UI (not baked text artifacts).
SH20 Before-after compare at identical living viewpoint, two actual finish presets, color temperature matched.

## Camera & metadata for every shot
scene_id, shot_id, local camera position xyz, target xyz, nominal lens/FOV, aspect, resolution, source .blend, rendered package, daylight state, texture pack version, asset license owner, image filename, alt text AR/EN, what it proves, reviewer score. Material comparison uses SAME camera and lighting. Render final via reliable antialias/denoise and inspect 100% zoom; no floating objects, repeated UVs, clipped railings or overbright glass. Composition must crop gracefully on 16:9 and 9:16 or create two purposeful crop variants.

## Camera movement storyboard
T01 Hero: very slow dolly/orbit until subject understood; CTA never hidden.
T02 Floor hover: slight silhouette highlight no cinematic cut.
T03 Floor select: 0.8–1.8s easing zoom/extract with stable orientation.
T04 Unit select: floor plate develops into cutaway and selected boundary glows delicately.
T05 Room enter: position and target animate via wall-safe path or match-cut, ending in visually beautiful frame.
T06 Material selection: room stays anchored to show clear before/after; no distractive camera.
T07 Back out: inverse of T05 and T04 where plausible, no memory loss.
T08 Amenities: elevator-inspired spatial focus to upper level, scene swap clearly contextual.
T09 Guided tour end: return to tower hero or finish board, no endless autoplay.

## Audio optional
If sound chosen, require original/legally licensed gentle warm architecture ambience; subtle no gimmicks, mute toggle, captions if voiceover, no audio autoplay on initial page.

DOCUMENT: research/RESEARCH_AND_SKILL_PROTOCOL.md
===========================================================================
# Mandatory deep research, skills, GitHub and tool acquisition playbook

## Research with evidence
Begin with a 60–120 question checklist categorized by architecture, climate, site, structural, fire/MEP, accessible design, pool, restaurant, shisha local regulations, web 3D, Blender, rendering, scene selection, materials, licensing, localization, WCAG, mobile and benchmarking. Research from official primary sources first; timestamp links; store in project_artifacts/RESEARCH_LOG.md. For each claim record question, link, publisher, publication/update date (if visible), interpretation, uncertainty, effect on design. Cross-verify high-stakes rules before relying on them. When source unavailable, state assumption, never invent an authority. A beautiful rendering does not constitute validated design approval.

## Architecture benchmarking
Inspect at least 3–5 publicly accessible premium 3D property exploration sites/demos for *interaction patterns* (not copying), and 3–5 climate-conscious residential buildings or modern Iraqi architecture examples for typology. Record what is transferable: building navigator, floor heatmap, isometric cutaway, gallery, finishes inspector, material board, day/night, 2D accessibility fallback, guided tour. Note mismatches with Basra context. If video/reel reference provided, extract navigation/storyboard and useful frames; don't pull production assets or logos from it.

## Technical alternatives
Compare Vite+React vs Next.js (SEO/localization/static hosting), R3F vs native Three (integration/optimization), Blender/Eevee/Cycles vs hybrid (quality/hardware), true realtime interior vs prerendered panorama (user choice requirements), GLB+Draco/Meshopt/KTX2 optimization, 2D backup. Use decision matrix: fidelity 30%, reliability 25%, performance 15%, integration 10%, maintenance 10%, free cost/license 10% (modify weights with rationale). Verify actual current versions and installation instructions; dependency scanning and lockfiles.

## Inspect existing environment and user skill installation first
1. Detect Windows 11 shell/PowerShell vs WSL, Python/pip/uv, Node/npm/pnpm, Git, Blender, FFmpeg, Python GPU packages, browser/test runner. Record versions.
2. Check project files and existing Claude Code skill directories, plugin registries, MCP servers and already installed commands; do not read secrets or unrelated personal data. Never install something duplicate or incompatibly layered.
3. Identify skills relevant to: creative web design, 3D/Blender automation, Three.js/R3F, browser testing, code quality, SVG/icon/typography, asset optimization, GitHub exploration, accessibility, architecture, source licensing, video storyboard if genuinely used.
4. A user's earlier preference explicitly includes `Lemo-Opuscar` (lemomo-ai/lemo-opuscar) for motion/video workflows. Check whether installed and applicable to optional promotional motion capture; **do not falsely make it a dependency for an interactive website** or download if irrelevant. If useful, inspect security/licenses/compatibility and apply only to promotional guided trailer.
5. Consider official/maintained tools before community skill wrappers: Blender Python API, Three.js/R3F/Drei, GSAP, Playwright, glTF Transform, Google Lighthouse, axe-core, typography/font tools, standard code architecture skill; versions and license check mandatory.
6. For each candidate third-party skill/repository, produce audit card: URL, organization, stars (if trustworthy), recent activity, license, exact version/ref, transitive dependencies, file read/write/network/shell scope, trust, evidence screenshots, alternatives, estimated value. Stars are NOT safety proof. Inspect scripts and prompts for prompt injection/credential theft/data exfiltration. Pin commit/semver if possible. Do not pipe remote script into shell (`curl | bash`), use official package managers, no elevated permissions unless essential and explicitly permitted.
7. Install only vetted relevant FREE tools, preferably inside isolated project (local node_modules, Python venv, pinned packages), document `SKILLS_AND_TOOLS_MANIFEST.md`, commands and reversal/removal. No paid subscription/auth tokens/telemetry without user's explicit consent.
8. If Claude cannot install tools (chat-only or permission constrained), specify exact commands for later and continue designing/building what can be built; never claim installed when not.

## Parallel agent use
If available, delegate bounded, read-only research tasks to subagents: architecture/urban rules, 3D pipeline, UI/UX, skills/security, testing/accessibility. Synthesize in main agent only after cross-check; do not allow simultaneous agents to edit same files without ownership rules. No uncontrolled recursive agent spawning, token waste or accidental cross-project browsing. Agent roles are optional, not an excuse to defer execution.

## Stop rules for research
Do not let endless skill hunting block a working vertical slice. Start implementation once minimum viable decisions are validated; schedule focused research for unknowns that actually change implementation. If a feature requires a questionable license/regulation, omit that risky representation and build an honest alternate.

## Mandatory research outputs
PROJECT_ARC_RESEARCH.md; REFERENCES_AND_LICENSES.csv; DESIGN_ALTERNATIVES.md; STACK_ADR.md; SKILLS_AND_TOOLS_MANIFEST.md; RISK_REGISTER.md; COST_AND_PERFORMANCE_BUDGET.md; DESIGN_ASSUMPTIONS.md. Include actual URLs and access dates, not vague "research completed" claims.

Their download page states documentation use restrictions. These references do not give permission for cloning their building.

These are research prompts; never manufacture legal approvals or professional certification. A concept may be speculative and must be labeled accordingly.

Using the user's screen-recording of an Instagram reel gives permission to inspect as visual inspiration, NOT permission to redistribute its images/architectural renderings as original site assets. Do not ship third-party protected visual works. Remove any embedded third-party branding from screenshots only if it is a legitimate reference illustration; no reference screenshot should appear in production marketing site.

DOCUMENT: ops/AUTONOMY_AND_RECOVERY.md
===========================================================================
# Autonomous execution, reboot and checkpoint protocol

## Authority and boundaries
Owner wants Claude to do all *ordinary project work* autonomously, including research, local coding, Blender tasks, installation of inspected free project-scoped dependencies, tests, repairs, documentation. Avoid repetitive questions about harmless aesthetic defaults. However explicit confirmation is still required for spending money/using paid APIs, publishing/deploying to a real external website, modifying OS/global policies, entering personal accounts, touching other projects, changing security settings, granting permission escalation, or contacting third parties. Never override safeguards with an auto-approve bypass.

## Checkpoint every substantial unit of work
Maintain /project_artifacts/PROGRESS_LOG.md with: timestamp, phase, achieved proof, next 3 tasks, blockers, commands that work, working source commit, critical model/render paths, active dependency versions, artifacts output, test state. Create /project_artifacts/RESUME_HERE.md with compact human-readable restart instructions updated after each milestone and before long renders. Use Git staged commits inside the new project (no remote push), and keep local backup of durable originals. Do not write checkpoints during half-written JSON or compile steps; atomic write-then-rename for derived state when possible.

## Power loss and stopped session
Computer power loss or user shutting down PC **stops actual execution**; Claude cannot continue on a powered-off computer unless separately configured reliable infrastructure exists. This protocol preserves reproducibility, not magic background work. On restart Claude reads RESUME_HERE.md and build/test logs, checks partial renders for corruption, resumes at last verified checkpoint. Blender can use checkpointed source .blend and chunked render outputs; do not promise completed work while machine is off.

## Render resilience
Auto-save .blend source and output manifests; use unique file naming and content hashes, avoid overwriting last good hero render until new version verified. If GPU render OOM, lower samples/resolution/denoiser memory or switch engine and record visual tradeoff, never claim unchanged quality. Reproducible scripts with seeded random choices. After interrupt, detect incomplete files and rerun only needed assets.

## Task scheduling
Operate in finite phases rather than a single fragile monolithic run. Keep every milestone buildable. Prefer focused 30–90 minute chunks if environment supports, but do not make time guarantees. When session/token limits approach, checkpoint RESUME_HERE.md with precise next actions so next session can pick up; no false background continuation. The user should only need to say "continue from RESUME_HERE.md" on a restarted session.

## Troubleshooting loop
One failing task: inspect error -> reproduce -> isolate -> smallest fix -> rerun automated tests -> capture evidence -> checkpoint. If failure repeats twice without new evidence, research alternative and adjust ADR. If a critical external paid dependency appears, replace it with free/open alternative or mark blocked; never sneakily buy it. If a weak scene looks toy-like, do not mark visual gate done; invest in source renders and shading or use honest hybrid.

## Completion proof
A runnable local URL/process status checked in runtime, commands, successful build result, representative E2E evidence, known limitations, where source and renders are located. Never assert a URL is live unless actual tested process is running. Notion of "all done" requires release gate evidence, not subjective optimism.

# Windows 11 Claude Code: zero-friction startup and local workflow

Suggested user action: put this ZIP in a new empty folder and let Claude Code open/read START_HERE.txt. Some interfaces cannot read ZIP contents directly; if so, extract it first. If only a chat attachment is supported, attach MASTER_PROMPT_COMPLETE.txt alone; it contains the instructions and embedded supporting docs. The user cannot expect a chat-only model to install Blender or run code without a connected computer/runtime.

Claude environment actions:
- Detect PowerShell, Git, Node, Python and Blender using standard version queries before installing anything; try binaries already in PATH. If not installed, choose a reputable official installer only with required authorization and no elevation bypass. Prefer Node dependencies in project and Python venv in project.
- Use `npm ci` where lockfile exists, or safe pinned installation when initializing; no `npm install -g` by default.
- Use Blender headless `blender -b source.blend --python script.py` only after `blender --version` confirms actual installation; do not assume binary name/path.
- Validate glTF assets and run browser tests; launch with `npm run dev` or exact package scripts actually configured, not random assumptions.
- Create optional `start-demo.ps1` with conservative commands, no disabling execution policy or mutating system settings. Explain Windows security warnings and do not ask for blanket PowerShell auto-execution bypass.
- Save work before large GPU renders and keep files within this project directory. Checkpoint after scene updates.
- If Blender is missing and installing it is blocked, work on licensed original scene alternatives or create generated proxy geometry in Three.js temporarily, clearly mark visual quality gate open until Blender-quality visuals available.
- Do not assume the user has a paid proprietary render service, cloud VM, licensed plugins or unlimited token budget. Prefer free local tools and reasonable rendering loads.

DOCUMENT: qa/TEST_STRATEGY.md
===========================================================================
# Comprehensive test strategy

## Test types
1. Schema validation: floor count, unique IDs, room data, area program, source alignment; units only floors 1–9, amenities only 10/11, consistent camera anchors, valid PBR paths and material-category restrictions.
2. Unit tests: package reducer, overrides, reset, undo/redo, share encoding, missing IDs, nulls, bilingual dictionary coverage.
3. Component tests: inspector, carousel, breadcrumbs, responsive modal, select menus, contrast and keyboard behavior.
4. End-to-end on desktop and mobile: tower -> floor -> apartment -> room -> change porcelain to oak allowed -> undo -> compare -> gallery -> summary -> export; amenities both; language switch; intro skip; accessibility list; 2D fallback; return and refresh.
5. Real GPU/browser tests: Three assets actually loaded, no context crash, scene raycast floor selection, texture color, day/night, xray visibility; stop-start animation and reverse transition; phone throttling.
6. Visual regression: hero/frame at all responsive viewports, floor cutaway, living material variants, modal states, RTL, overflow, mobile menus. Capture screenshots from stable same OS/browser and deterministic camera seed.
7. Performance: first load, lazy loading, 10 consecutive floor switches, 20 material switches, repeated room changes, GPU memory and heap growth, FPS/long tasks, asset transfer budget, min/average device classes.
8. Data/copy integrity: fictional disclaimers everywhere applicable, localized safety notes, no fake pricing/address/official supplier/certifications.
9. Security: validate deep-link query, sanitize inputs, no token uploads; no unauthorized external calls; dependency audit and license manifest.
10. Resilience: network offline during loads, missing GLB, failed texture, invalid share link, unsupported WebGL, context loss, device rotate, reload, reduced motion and battery quality tier.

## Required artifacts
/tests/unit, /tests/e2e, browser screenshots and trace recordings (if tools available), typecheck/lint logs, Lighthouse/axe report when possible, tester's issue list and remediation evidence. If browser automation unavailable, manually inspect and record clearly what was and wasn't tested. All P0 fail gates block release unless honest accessible fallback is working.

DOCUMENT: qa/USER_JOURNEYS_GHERKIN.md
===========================================================================
# User journeys for Claude to implement as automated functional tests

Feature: Tower floor picking
Scenario: Building has 12 real accessible floor targets
Given the exterior scene has loaded
When I open floor picker
Then Ground, residential floors 01 through 09, Wellness 10 and Dining 11 are available
And floor selection shows a coherent correctly highlighted level
And selecting floor 10 never shows unit A or B.

Feature: Apartment room and finish editing
Scenario: User changes actual floor material, navigates and returns
Given I have selected Floor 04 and Unit 04A
And I entered the living room
When I choose the flooring surface
And I switch from Ivory Porcelain to Engineered Oak in an allowed dry interior
Then the real 3D floor texture/roughness changes in that room
And material information shows concept sample status
And switching room and returning preserves Engineered Oak
And Undo restores Ivory Porcelain.

Feature: Material package coherence
Scenario: Applying preset updates the correct scene and board
Given Unit 04A with an overridden kitchen cabinet
When I choose Walnut Harmony preset
Then all intended slot IDs update to the package
And the UI shows which overrides were overwritten
And the finish board lists the true effective choices
And gallery stills not matching those exact choices are marked representative.

Feature: Amenity exploration
Scenario: Pool and restaurant are not apartments
Given I am at tower exterior
When I select 10
Then I can choose gym/pool/wellness zones, not a residential unit
When I return and select 11
Then I can choose café/restaurant/panoramic terrace
And shisha is clearly described as concept/subject to approval if shown.

Feature: Responsive localization
Scenario: Arabic visitor from narrow phone
Given reduced-motion is enabled and locale is Arabic
When I launch on a 360px viewport
Then the primary Explore control is in view
And floor selection works via tap, not hover
And material inspector is usable as a bottom sheet
And numeric labels, RTL and print summary remain readable.

Feature: Honest inquiry
Scenario: No backend is configured
Given I have customized Unit 04A
When I fill an inquiry form and submit
Then validation runs
And a truthful local-only confirmation appears
And no success message claims contact with a real company.

Feature: Graceful failure
Scenario: WebGL unavailable
Given GPU WebGL is disabled or a scene asset fails
When I open building explorer
Then I see a useful 2D floor selector/gallery fallback
And can inspect summary and finish info without a blank page.

DOCUMENT: qa/TEST_CASES_GUIDE.md
===========================================================================
# Test trace and deliverable evidence guide
For each e2e: record step, input, expected behavior, actual result, screenshot or trace path, bug ID, retest. Do not mark a test passed simply because code exists. If automated interaction with WebGL meshes is flaky, expose semantic UI floor list that reaches same state and test that, then separately manually confirm actual raycasts via visual/browser evidence.

Visual acceptance: hero fills 16:9 and 9:16 without clipping; building silhouette 12 levels consistent; exposed slab not hollow; architecture/no floating floor; textured balcony and main facade; luxury room renders believable; 3D material updates obvious; label not occluded; compare uses same exposure; Arabic typography no disconnected letters; polished loading and empty/error states; photographic gallery captions honest; mobile controls not overlapping bottom browser chrome.

Performance acceptance: graph load time per scene, active renderer memory, material swap time at cached state, network bytes, LCP and CLS where measurable. No fabricated FPS claims. Gate only on confirmed metrics or recorded environment limitations.

--- specs/PERFORMANCE_NO_LAG_CONTRACT.md ---
# PERFORMANCE NO-LAG CONTRACT — v2.0 — MANDATORY P0 RELEASE GATE

## Mission and definition of smooth
The user's hard requirement is that a prospect opening an ordinary link on a mobile phone, iPad, laptop, or desktop experiences a smooth, intuitive, visually stunning website, **not a huge 3D download, stalled loading bar, overheating phone, crashed tab, input lag, freezing transition, or opaque black canvas**. Performance is not a finishing detail; it determines art direction, scene architecture, animation, data shape, UX and release. Optimize for a mid-range phone with ordinary mobile data *before* desktop visual polish. Professional execution MUST measure in actual browsers, not promise 60 FPS from intuition.

ABSOLUTE TRUTH RULE: No honest developer can guarantee zero bugs, no jitter, or compatibility with every device/network. Instead implement a measurable service target, extensive real-device/browser QA, graceful fallback, and transparent release report. Never falsely claim measurements or testing.

## Priority order, if visual polish conflicts
1. Consistently usable and responsive input/clear copy;
2. Fast first useful paint and access to building/floor/unit information;
3. Correct navigation and state preservation;
4. Smooth 3D camera and surface changes on target hardware;
5. Premium believable material/light rendering;
6. Optional weather particles, film effects, shadows, animation, reflections, audio.
NO postprocessing/effect is indispensable. A buttery-smooth low/medium tier is better than intermittent photoreal freezes.

## Explicit numerical goals: targets to test, not false guarantees
Google Core Web Vitals, 75th percentile of field visits (when enough genuine visits exist): LCP <=2.5s, INP <=200ms, CLS <=0.1, segmented mobile/desktop. These are CWV indicators, not equivalent to 3D fully loaded time. During local throttled tests, record measured results and equipment rather than claim a universal passing score. The initial semantic hero (logo, title, compelling *optimized* poster, Explore control) must appear before a heavy 3D runtime is needed.
- Usability: controls respond with visible pressed/selected feedback without waiting for a 3D fetch; never delay a click silently.
- Under realistic mid-tier hardware, working 3D interactivity target >=30 FPS during representative rotation/walkthrough, >=45 FPS on a reasonably capable tested desktop/tablet when feasible. Target smooth 60 on strong hardware; do not force 60 for all devices. Capture median, p5, p1 frame rates, frame-time p95/p99, stutter count and worst-case spikes, and indicate scene/quality tier.
- Aim interaction visual confirmation <100ms before async work begins; no blocking main thread longer than 100ms on common interactions; preferably <50ms where feasible. For on-demand GL assets always show progress, retry and escape.
- Test 10 rapid floor selections, 20 material swaps, 15 room changes, 12 environment changes and 3 complete demo loops for memory leaks and freeze regression. After returning to start, memory/GPU object counts should stabilize within normal caches and documented tolerances; monotonic unbounded growth fails.
- Set *initial aspirational* transfer budgets before prototype: critical first paint HTML/CSS/JS/poster <= ~1.5MB transferred on modern compression; initial viewer scene assets <= ~3-6MB for low/mid tier in first interaction; individual interior/amenity scene bundles preferably <= ~3-8MB low/mid and <= ~8-15MB high. These are starting budgets, not immutable promises; measure high-res art and revise with evidence. Zero blocking video and zero interior/pool/restaurant bundles on landing load. Size the optimized LCP hero image by viewport. NO 100MB upfront download.
- For slow networks ensure text/site overview is usable in seconds even if 3D takes longer; set a reasonable viewer timeout and use poster/2D controls on failure. Include a quick-first-interaction metric (e.g. "time to select a floor" under the test network conditions) and cold vs warm cache metrics.
- Avoid unnecessary ongoing GPU activity while idle. Pause rendering when tab hidden, battery/low-power constraints, reduced motion and when scene is not visible. Never bind all controls to continuous requestAnimationFrame without necessity.

## Three-stage launch: never load entire world immediately
STAGE A — Critical HTML shell: brand, heading, condensed 2D tower overview, poster, accessible floor controls, AR/EN selector, and immediate 'Explore' button; server-render/static HTML where sensible. This stage MUST render without WebGL/Blender.
STAGE B — Exterior interactive progressive: on observed user intent, fetch one optimized facade/glb, 1-2 essential environment assets, minimal shaders. Show poster until first valid frame, never a black rectangle. Load optional closeups and higher LOD after viewer is useful; preserve floor selection across upgrades.
STAGE C — On-demand zones: one selected floor/unit/room and nearby adjacent scenes, then amenity scenes ONLY when navigated to. Prefetch conservatively on idle/good network; abort irrelevant downloads when user switches quickly. Never hold all 18 furnished apartments, 5 interiors, gym, pool, restaurant and four weather variants in GPU memory simultaneously.

## GPU/asset preparation pipeline (Blender -> Web)
- One canonical structural source mesh and transforms. Split by *semantic selection boundaries* (floor, room, selectable surface) and render/culling boundaries, but intelligently merge permanently static pieces within each region.
- Export glTF/GLB with correct real-world scale (meters), normals, tangents, meaningful pivots, UVs, consistent color management, named object IDs and floor/unit metadata. Run glTF validator. Remove unused meshes, cameras, hidden duplicates, non-visible interior behind opaque walls, orphan materials and baked Cycles-only nodes unsupported in realtime glTF.
- Geometric LOD and material LOD: low/mid/high. Reduce polygon count, bake details to PBR textures/normal maps, reuse meshes via instancing where semantic picking can still resolve original floor/unit ID. Occlusion/visibility culling and frustum culling; don't draw hidden interior from external tour. Avoid per-frame expensive raycasts; raycast only on pointer events or throttled on move with coarse targets.
- Texture compression via KTX2/Basis Universal where supported; test decoding on Safari/iOS, Android and desktop; fallback compressed WebP/AVIF/JPEG/PNG as appropriate, never ship original 8K PNGs by default. Provide properly sized mips, correct sRGB/linear channels, reasonable anisotropy, normal map scale and PBR map packing. Typical default textures 512px-1K low, 1K-2K high for critical objects; 4K only when measured and justified. Use atlases and shared samplers where effective; avoid texture duplication from every variant.
- Mesh/geometry compression: compare meshopt and Draco on actual output, browser decoding time, implementation and toolchain; choose measured winner, document required decoders. Ensure worker dependencies/WASM files are shipped at known versions, cached, and served with correct MIME. Do not stack compression schemes blindly.
- Consider baked GI/lightmaps/reflection probes, inexpensive analytic dynamic sun for weather/time, limited shadow maps with cascades only where needed. In low tier disable/refine expensive SSR, SSAO, volumetrics, bloom, realtime reflection, numerous lights, alpha particles, high-resolution shadows and high sample anisotropy. Never make dynamic day/night depend on recalculating realtime global illumination on every slider move.
- Material swap: cache common textures, update targeted mesh/material uniforms without replacing whole GLB, debounce rapid changes, upload on idle/preload nearest swatches, dispose only unreferenced resources. Separate source material catalog ID from GPU texture instance; avoid clone leaks.
- Deterministic original assets and provenance/rights. Rendering software can be slow; ship static render plus functional 3D tour when realtime visual fidelity has limits. Don't falsify gallery image matches current custom selections.

## Runtime and adaptive quality controller
Quality tiers: 2D Lite -> Low -> Balanced -> High -> Ultra (opt-in); detect device capabilities via features/performance measurements, not UA only, screen size or guessed GPU name. Start conservatively then increase only after stable measurements. "Auto" should be default with user override and saved preference. Debounce/hysteresis prevent tier oscillation. Quality adjustments must NOT move furniture, remove doors, break hit targets, reset material choices, change room geometry, remove accessibility semantics or hide key content.
- Sample moving window of frame times, memory clues where available, CPU long tasks, canvas dimensions, devicePixelRatio, network hint where available, battery saver when available and thermal symptoms (infer only from sustained slowdown, not invented API).
- Initial viewport DPR cap by tier: Lite=no canvas, Low~0.75-1.0, Balanced~1.0-1.4, High~1.5-2.0 subject to measured hardware. Decouple CSS layout pixels from backing render resolution. Resize carefully without expensive repeated render-target recreation on keyboard opening.
- Example degrade order: turn off particles/audio visualizers -> postprocess off -> reduce reflection/shadow quality -> reduce DPR -> select lower model/texture LOD -> reduce vegetation/props -> pause nonessential simulations -> show Lite mode if GPU fails. Upgrade only after sustained headroom and never during a camera animation or active user swipe.
- Quality control is a distinct domain service with telemetry callbacks, not a hand-wavy `if mobile then low`. Implement per-zone budgets because exterior, cutaway, furnished interior, pool and restaurant differ.
- Render-on-demand when idle; invalidate only on user interactions, animated camera transitions, environment interpolation or material change. If an intro/guide animation occurs, run scoped loop and stop on completion; maintain maximum dt to avoid jumping after tab resumes.

## Loading, cancellation, recovery
- Download real progress if measurable; avoid '98%' fake spinner; display stage text and estimated size where known. Persistent Back/Exit to 2D, Cancel tour and Retry.
- AbortController / task sequence tokens protect against stale asset response overwriting newer floor choice; race: F03 click then F09 click => F09 always wins. Unmount/cancel disposable workers, animation timelines, renderers. Guard rapid transitions from double-click, multitouch, back-navigation and browser history.
- Handle `webglcontextlost`, `webglcontextrestored`, GPU OOM, loader error, corrupted GLB, decoder missing, HTTP 404/500, poor connection, offline, low storage, JS error boundary, invalid scene ID, invalid saved state, unsupported texture format and zero viewport. Restore state when possible, then fallback to semantic 2D image+DOM floor/unit/room controls. No infinite reload crash loop.
- If 3D cannot render, the visitor can still browse building info, choose a floor and unit, inspect static room images/finish specs, compare selection, and contact via demo-safe workflow. Include a visible small '3D unavailable / Switch to Lite' action, not a blocked blank canvas.
- Media must provide alt text, lazy gallery loading, responsive `srcset/sizes` and dimensions, smart prefetch of next image, and safe animated image codecs. No automatic sound, autoplay video or delayed full-screen giant animation.

## Network, CDN, caching and deployment-readiness
- Immutable versioned fingerprinted asset URLs + long-lived immutable cache for GLB/KTX2/render assets; short cache for mutable app/data. HTTP compression (Brotli/gzip), modern transport, range support if useful and verified, CDN edge assets optional if allowed. Don't require API paid services. Ensure deterministic cache invalidation when model version updates; do not cache nonexistent/invalid files as good versions.
- Use consent-aware service worker only if truly beneficial and correct; avoid stale HTML/asset pair. Test Safari back-forward, reload under offline, slow cache, change of locale, new release, expired references, and reconnect. Progressive app shell available with accessible content even when asset CDN unreachable.
- No third-party analytics or service calls without explicit informed choice; if local diagnostics used, clearly identify no sensitive data. Never report performance metrics that weren't measured.

## Practical profiling requirements
Use browser Performance recorder, GPU/resource inspector when available, React profiler, network waterfall, bundle analyzer, Core Web Vitals tooling, Lighthouse mobile+desktop, Playwright traces and original device test. Record browser build, device, OS, battery state if known, network conditions, viewport, resolution, quality tier, cold/warm load, LCP/INP/CLS if observable, FPS median/p5, peak GPU/JS heap approximation when available, transferred MB, time until first interactive floor, and screenshot. If a metric isn't available, mark unmeasured and explain.
Prototype first with one exterior floor selection and one hero interior swappable floor; performance gate before adding amenity/particles/fancy transitions. Profile each completed vertical slice, not once at the end. 90% fidelity with 100% usable interactions beats a showcase image on an unusable website.

## Performance acceptance decision
P0 FAIL if any target test environment gives a completely blocked site/no fallback, runaway memory, unusable floor selection, regular multi-second input stalls or crash; P1 FAIL if user cannot independently turn effects down, page shifts repeatedly, or third-party fonts/assets block usability. If a performance target is missed, triage and simplify; do not fake a green report. See QA/PERFORMANCE_E2E_ACCEPTANCE.md for reproducible tests.

## Verified reference sources (check latest documentation during execution)
- Google Web Vitals: https://web.dev/articles/vitals
- Google LCP optimization: https://web.dev/articles/optimize-lcp
- Three.js optimize lots of objects: https://threejs.org/manual/pages/optimize-lots-of-objects.html
- Three.js KTX2Loader: https://threejs.org/docs/pages/KTX2Loader.html
- MDN context loss: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLCanvasElement/webglcontextlost_event


--- specs/RESPONSIVE_INPUT_ACCESSIBILITY_CONTRACT.md ---
# UNIVERSAL RESPONSIVE INTERACTION CONTRACT — PHONE • TABLET • LAPTOP • DESKTOP

## Product promise
One shared site link must be understandable and operable across a broad, explicitly tested device matrix: iPhone Safari, Android Chrome, iPadOS Safari with touch/stylus/trackpad, desktop Chrome/Edge, desktop Safari and Firefox. Older/unsupported 3D browsers get graceful Lite UI. Do NOT claim universal compatibility without real testing; include a compatibility page in final delivery listing verified setups and fallbacks.

## One information architecture, several interaction modes
The complete journey must be possible with: mouse, trackpad, touch, keyboard, accessibility focus and a pure HTML 2D floor list when 3D cannot operate. Hover is visual enhancement, never sole way to learn floor or material information. Semantic DOM elements (buttons/links, aria-labels, visible focus ring, screen-reader text) shadow 3D hotspots and floor meshes. Focus order follows current modal/dialog; when dialog closes focus returns to activating element. No input trap, no absent Escape/Back button. Every nondecorative image has meaningful alt or transcript. Color + shape/label indicates selection; distinguish sold/reserved/demo statuses with text and high contrast.

## Viewport regimes (design with container queries, not fixed brittle breakpoints)
- Extra compact: >=320 CSS px with safe-area left/right and no sideways scroll. This is baseline information usability; low-end may use Lite mode.
- Phones 360/375/390/412/430, portrait and landscape; iOS Safari address-bar collapse/expand, on-screen keyboard, overscroll, notch/dynamic island and home indicator; Android Chrome gesture bar and varied font scales. Test 200% browser text zoom where possible.
- Small tablet 600-820 portrait; iPad 768/834/1024 in landscape; pointer+touch mixed; split view/multitasking resized viewport (not just screen resolution).
- Laptops 1024/1280/1366/1440 with browser zoom 100 and 125%; desktop 1600/1920/2560; high-density/ultrawide without stretched text or tiny buttons.
- Retina devices must NEVER force framebuffer DPR equal physical DPR if that causes lag; CSS pixel UI remains crisp while rendered 3D resolution is adaptive.

## Screen layouts
Mobile: sticky small logo, language and menu; floating 3D controls in safe corners; scene occupies available viewport with unobstructed subject; bottom sheet for floor/unit/room/material options, swipeable sheet but also explicit Open/Close controls; horizontal material swatch scroller; floor list accessible via bottom drawer; light text, large targets, tap-only alternate. Use `100dvh` with tested fallback when appropriate and safe-area insets. When keyboard opens on inquiry form, avoid jumping camera, hiding submit, viewport recenter. Exit and Home always visible.
Tablet: split scene with collapsible left/right drawer based on orientation; touch/pencil target sizing even if desktop-like layout; careful Apple Pencil hover availability not assumed. Compact controls for notch and landscape.
Laptop/Desktop: ample 3D area and persistent side rail for contextual metadata, breadcrumbs and comparators, optional expanded property cards, mouse wheel scoped to canvas only when intentional; page scroll remains usable outside canvas. Hover preview floor and click to select; virtual tour controls documented unobtrusively.
All devices: no tiny 3D-only hit targets. Minimum usable tap targets 44 x 44 CSS px as a target (prefer 48) with spacing so adjacent actions are distinct. Actual geometry hit regions should grow invisibly without mis-picking neighbor floors. Visible button labels matter: avoid icon-only mystery controls; tooltips not required to operate mobile.

## Gesture specification and conflicts
- Single finger on active 3D viewer: orbit/look only after entering dedicated viewer, otherwise page scrolling. Use explicit mode hint and avoid hijacking vertical scroll while reading floor info. Explicit close and gesture tips. Disable pinch page zoom only inside a specific 3D gesture surface if necessary, not globally; accessible browser zoom remains possible.
- Two finger: pinch to zoom within constrained viewer. Two fingers on 2D plan can pan/zoom the plan; do not cause browser window to move unexpectedly.
- Single tap: select object/floor/room; two quick taps are never interpreted as two navigation commands. Deliberate drag vs click threshold avoids accidental room entry.
- Keyboard: arrows, Tab, Enter, Space, Esc/Back, WASD for opt-in walk mode, +/- for zoom where expected; show shortcut help. Prevent WASD from capturing keystrokes while input/textarea or screen reader focus is active.
- Stylus, trackpad, touch+keyboard mixed: detect actual pointer event behavior, not by viewport width alone. Pointer lock is optional Desktop only and requires a clear click; never default or trap.
- If gyro/DeviceOrientation is proposed, make it *optional* with explicit permission and a no-gyro alternative; not required for core experience.

## Spatial UX and accessibility
Every 3D scene also has clear DOM navigation: Building floors -> Units -> Rooms -> Surfaces -> Material choices. All names and prices marked Demo; valid link and explicit AR/EN. Camera motion should be subtle, skippable, and honor `prefers-reduced-motion`. Provide a Persistent 'Reduce Effects / Lite Mode' action accessible by keyboard and touch.
Do not make people dizzy: avoid camera roll, sudden acceleration, rapid orbit, endless spin, surprise zoom/pointer lock. Limit walk speed and motion blur. Add field-of-view defaults 60-75 degrees as starting hypothesis and allow comfort option; adjust to actual interiors. For reduced motion use instant cuts/dissolves and static backdrop in place of scripted flythrough.
Audio disabled by default; accessible mute button if opt-in. Use text and optional caption for audio cues. No flashing light/strobe effects, especially lightning. Sky lightning can be absent; weather uses low-motion lighting, no flicker.
When comparing finishes, the before-after slider must have a keyboard-operable alternative, explicit labels and textual descriptions. Do not show only color swatches; name material and include accessible specification values.

## Localization and typography
Arabic is first-class RTL; English equivalent LTR, with direction controlled per subtree not globally flattening CSS. Numerals/measurements/room IDs/phone format remain legible; avoid bidirectional control abuse. Proper line wrapping for Arabic; variable Latin unit names isolated by bdi-like semantic elements where appropriate. Fonts must be legally usable and loading must not block content. Avoid 10+ font weights. Responsive font sizes with CSS clamp, comfortable line height, contrast and meaningful hierarchy. Long Arabic strings, no truncation of critical settings and prices. Audit: safe area and top-bottom HUD in both languages; all form validation and errors localized.

## User error prevention and recovery
Keep browser Back semantics and custom Back controls coherent; deep link after reload returns the same selected scene if available. Rapid gesture, browser tab hidden, screen rotation and network reconnect never corrupt floor/unit/material selections. Preserve choices during locale switch, weather switch, quality-tier switch and 3D->2D switch. Reset is explicit and offers Undo when destructive. Display quick guided option for first visit, dismissible forever in local demo mode; avoid blocking 12-step onboarding.
Share links should encode stable project/floor/unit/room/finish/weather metadata within reasonable URL size, validate and sanitize query. UI must never silently claim persistent server-side saves if only local storage works. Provide privacy-safe locally stored presets and explicit Clear saved preferences.

## Real test grid and release evidence
Use data/DEVICE_AND_NETWORK_TEST_MATRIX.csv; test at least one real iPhone and Android, iPad or tablet, and a non-gaming laptop if available. Desktop browser emulation alone DOES NOT validate mobile GPU/WebGL, Safari touch or real thermal performance. If devices unavailable mark NOT VERIFIED; still deliver Lite fallback and documented limitations.
Each target viewport: landing title not clipped; tower selectable; floor list usable; room entered; both finish metadata and changing live swatch work; gallery and comparisons function; time/weather/season controls open without overlay obstruction; walk mode is operable or clear teleport alternative shown; back/reset/share/pdf path usable. Screenshot and screen-record samples for portrait/landscape, RTL/LTR and low quality. Use Lighthouse accessibility + axe when feasible; manual keyboard and touch audit obligatory.

## Compatibility acceptance
Release blocking defects: inability to access floor/unit/room on tested phone, overflow hiding essential controls, blank WebGL with no Lite fallback, touch scroll trap, text under status bar, modal behind keyboard, missing keyboard exit, unreadable RTL, and fatal crash after rotation/back navigation. Nonblocking minor animation artifacts must be recorded and prioritized.

Sources: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/%40media/prefers-reduced-motion ; https://web.dev/articles/vitals .


--- specs/ENVIRONMENT_TIME_WEATHER_SEASONS.md ---
# REALTIME ENVIRONMENT LAB — 4 SEASONS × WEATHER × TIME OF DAY

## Vision
The user may interactively change hour, sun position, moonlit/night ambience, sky, outdoor weather and season *without reloading the building and without resetting the selected floor/unit/room or finishes*. This is a compelling premium demo differentiator, not a meteorology claim. The geographic atmosphere should feel plausible for Basra, Iraq; real weather data and precise solar calculations are optional if independently validated; never invent live meteorological conditions or represent a virtual storm as actual current Basra weather.

## Interaction surface
Provide a top-right `Environment` compact launcher in tower view; in small screens a bottom sheet. Controls:
(1) `Time`: slider 00:00–23:59, accessible keyboard arrows and editable preset values; fast presets Sunrise / Midday / Golden Hour / Sunset / Night; optional `Follow Local Time` only when timezone and site/date logic are defined and marked simulated; 12/24h notation localized.
(2) `Weather`: Clear / Partly Cloudy / Overcast / Light Rain / Dust/Haze / Windy. Optional heat shimmer only on high tier and subtle. Offer Rain-on-exterior without flooding all interiors; climate realism matters. Never use thunderstorms/lightning by default.
(3) `Season`: Spring / Summer / Autumn / Winter. Spring changes greenery/pollen visually where appropriate, Summer brighter harsher solar light and faded warm vegetation, Autumn subdued landscape, Winter softer sun/cooler lighting but still Basra-like. NO snow blanket, snowy palms or alpine mountains as default because inaccurate. Optional stylistic 'fantasy mode' only explicitly labeled if ever added.
(4) `Quality`: Auto/Low/Balanced/High/Ultra separate from environment; Save favorite combination; Reset to realistic default (clear afternoon/summer or user-chosen documented standard).
(5) Optional `A/B Atmosphere Compare` two-snapshot split before/after without rendering two separate complex real-time canvases on low hardware.

## State model and non-negotiable invariants
